     1	# `etuples`
     2	
     3	[![Build Status](https://travis-ci.org/pythological/etuples.svg?branch=master)](https://travis-ci.org/pythological/etuples) [![Coverage Status](https://coveralls.io/repos/github/pythological/etuples/badge.svg?branch=master)](https://coveralls.io/github/pythological/etuples?branch=master) [![PyPI](https://img.shields.io/pypi/v/etuples)](https://pypi.org/project/etuples/)
     4	
     5	Python [S-expression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-expression) emulation using tuple-like objects.
     6	
     7	## Examples
     8	
     9	`etuple`s are like tuples:
    10	
    11	```python
    12	>>> from operator import add
    13	>>> from etuples import etuple, etuplize
    14	
    15	>>> et = etuple(add, 1, 2)
    16	>>> et
    17	ExpressionTuple((<built-in function add>, 1, 2))
    18	
    19	>>> from IPython.lib.pretty import pprint
    20	>>> pprint(et)
    21	e(<function _operator.add(a, b, /)>, 1, 2)
    22	
    23	>>> et[0:2]
    24	ExpressionTuple((<built-in function add>, 1))
    25	```
    26	
    27	`etuple`s can also be evaluated:
    28	
    29	```python
    30	>>> et.eval_obj
    31	3
    32	```
    33	
    34	Evaluated `etuple`s are cached:
    35	```python
    36	>>> et = etuple(add, "a", "b")
    37	>>> et.eval_obj
    38	'ab'
    39	
    40	>>> et.eval_obj is et.eval_obj
    41	True
    42	```
    43	
    44	Reconstructed `etuple`s and their evaluation results are preserved across tuple operations:
    45	```python
    46	>>> et_new = (et[0],) + et[1:]
    47	>>> et_new is et
    48	True
    49	>>> et_new.eval_obj is et.eval_obj
    50	True
    51	```
    52	
    53	`rator`, `rands`, and `apply` will return the operator, the operands, and apply the operation to the operands:
    54	```python
    55	>>> from etuples import rator, rands, apply
    56	>>> et = etuple(add, 1, 2)
    57	
    58	>>> rator(et)
    59	<built-in function add>
    60	
    61	>>> rands(et)
    62	ExpressionTuple((1, 2))
    63	
    64	>>> apply(rator(et), rands(et))
    65	3
    66	```
    67	
    68	
    69	`rator` and `rands` are [`multipledispatch`](https://github.com/mrocklin/multipledispatch) functions that can be extended to handle arbitrary objects:
    70	```python
    71	from etuples.core import ExpressionTuple
    72	from collections.abc import Sequence
    73	
    74	
    75	class Node:
    76	    def __init__(self, rator, rands):
    77	        self.rator, self.rands = rator, rands
    78	
    79	    def __eq__(self, other):
    80	        return self.rator == other.rator and self.rands == other.rands
    81	
    82	
    83	class Operator:
    84	    def __init__(self, op_name):
    85	        self.op_name = op_name
    86	
    87	    def __call__(self, *args):
    88	        return Node(Operator(self.op_name), args)
    89	
    90	    def __repr__(self):
    91	        return self.op_name
    92	
    93	    def __eq__(self, other):
    94	        return self.op_name == other.op_name
    95	
    96	
    97	rands.add((Node,), lambda x: x.rands)
    98	rator.add((Node,), lambda x: x.rator)
    99	
   100	
   101	@apply.register(Operator, (Sequence, ExpressionTuple))
   102	def apply_Operator(rator, rands):
   103	    return Node(rator, rands)
   104	```
   105	
   106	```python
   107	>>> mul_op, add_op = Operator("*"), Operator("+")
   108	>>> mul_node = Node(mul_op, [1, 2])
   109	>>> add_node = Node(add_op, [mul_node, 3])
   110	```
   111	
   112	`etuplize` will convert non-tuple objects into their corresponding `etuple` form:
   113	```python
   114	>>> et = etuplize(add_node)
   115	>>> pprint(et)
   116	e(+, e(*, 1, 2), 3)
   117	
   118	>>> et.eval_obj is add_node
   119	True
   120	```
   121	
   122	`etuplize` can also do shallow object-to-`etuple` conversions:
   123	```python
   124	>>> et = etuplize(add_node, shallow=True)
   125	>>> pprint(et)
   126	e(+, <__main__.Node at 0x7f347361a080>, 3)
   127	```
   128	
   129	## Installation
   130	
   131	Using `pip`:
   132	```bash
   133	pip install etuples
   134	```
   135	
   136	To install from source:
   137	```bash
   138	git clone git@github.com:pythological/etuples.git
   139	cd etuples
   140	pip install -r requirements.txt
   141	```
   142	
   143	Tests can be run with the provided `Makefile`:
   144	```bash
   145	make check
   146	```
   147

     1	import inspect
     2	import reprlib
     3	
     4	import toolz
     5	
     6	from collections.abc import Sequence
     7	
     8	
     9	etuple_repr = reprlib.Repr()
    10	etuple_repr.maxstring = 100
    11	etuple_repr.maxother = 100
    12	
    13	
    14	class InvalidExpression(Exception):
    15	    """An exception indicating that an `ExpressionTuple` is not a valid [S-]expression.
    16	
    17	    This exception is raised when an attempt is made to evaluate an
    18	    `ExpressionTuple` that does not have a valid operator (e.g. not a
    19	    `callable`).
    20	
    21	    """
    22	
    23	
    24	class KwdPair(object):
    25	    """A class used to indicate a keyword + value mapping.
    26	
    27	    TODO: Could subclass `ast.keyword`.
    28	
    29	    """
    30	
    31	    __slots__ = ("arg", "value")
    32	
    33	    def __init__(self, arg, value):
    34	        assert isinstance(arg, str)
    35	        self.arg = arg
    36	        self.value = value
    37	
    38	    @property
    39	    def eval_obj(self):
    40	        return KwdPair(self.arg, getattr(self.value, "eval_obj", self.value))
    41	
    42	    def __repr__(self):
    43	        return f"{self.__class__.__name__}({repr(self.arg)}, {repr(self.value)})"
    44	
    45	    def __str__(self):
    46	        return f"{self.arg}={self.value}"
    47	
    48	    def _repr_pretty_(self, p, cycle):
    49	        p.text(str(self))
    50	
    51	    def __eq__(self, other):
    52	        return (
    53	            type(self) == type(other)
    54	            and self.arg == other.arg
    55	            and self.value == other.value
    56	        )
    57	
    58	    def __hash__(self):
    59	        return hash((type(self), self.arg, self.value))
    60	
    61	
    62	class ExpressionTuple(Sequence):
    63	    """A tuple-like object that represents an expression.
    64	
    65	    This object caches the return value resulting from evaluation of the
    66	    expression it represents.  Likewise, it holds onto the "parent" expression
    67	    from which it was derived (e.g. as a slice), if any, so that it can
    68	    preserve the return value through limited forms of concatenation/cons-ing
    69	    that would reproduce the parent expression.
    70	
    71	    TODO: Should probably use weakrefs for that.
    72	    """
    73	
    74	    __slots__ = ("_eval_obj", "_tuple", "_parent")
    75	    null = object()
    76	
    77	    def __new__(cls, seq=None, **kwargs):
    78	
    79	        # XXX: This doesn't actually remove the entry from the kwargs
    80	        # passed to __init__!
    81	        # It does, however, remove it for the check below.
    82	        kwargs.pop("eval_obj", None)
    83	
    84	        if seq is not None and not kwargs and type(seq) == cls:
    85	            return seq
    86	
    87	        res = super().__new__(cls)
    88	
    89	        return res
    90	
    91	    def __init__(self, seq=None, **kwargs):
    92	        """Create an expression tuple.
    93	
    94	        If the keyword 'eval_obj' is given, the `ExpressionTuple`'s
    95	        evaluated object is set to the corresponding value.
    96	        XXX: There is no verification/check that the arguments evaluate to the
    97	        user-specified 'eval_obj', so be careful.
    98	        """
    99	
   100	        _eval_obj = kwargs.pop("eval_obj", self.null)
   101	        etuple_kwargs = tuple(KwdPair(k, v) for k, v in kwargs.items())
   102	
   103	        if seq:
   104	            self._tuple = tuple(seq) + etuple_kwargs
   105	        else:
   106	            self._tuple = etuple_kwargs
   107	
   108	        # TODO: Consider making these a weakrefs.
   109	        self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
   110	        self._parent = None
   111	
   112	    @property
   113	    def eval_obj(self):
   114	        """Return the evaluation of this expression tuple.
   115	
   116	        Warning: If the evaluation value isn't cached, it will be evaluated
   117	        recursively.
   118	
   119	        """
   120	        if len(self._tuple) == 0:
   121	            raise InvalidExpression()
   122	
   123	        if self._eval_obj is not self.null:
   124	            return self._eval_obj
   125	        else:
   126	            op = self._tuple[0]
   127	            op = getattr(op, "eval_obj", op)
   128	
   129	            if not callable(op):
   130	                raise InvalidExpression()
   131	
   132	            evaled_args = [getattr(i, "eval_obj", i) for i in self._tuple[1:]]
   133	            arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), evaled_args)
   134	            evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
   135	            evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])
   136	
   137	            try:
   138	                op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
   139	            except ValueError:
   140	                # This handles some builtin function types
   141	                _eval_obj = op(*(evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in evaled_kwargs]))
   142	            else:
   143	                op_args = op_sig.bind(
   144	                    *evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in evaled_kwargs}
   145	                )
   146	                op_args.apply_defaults()
   147	
   148	                _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)
   149	
   150	            # assert not isinstance(_eval_obj, ExpressionTuple)
   151	
   152	            self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
   153	            return self._eval_obj
   154	
   155	    @eval_obj.setter
   156	    def eval_obj(self, obj):
   157	        raise ValueError("Value of evaluated expression cannot be set!")
   158	
   159	    def __add__(self, x):
   160	        res = self._tuple + x
   161	        if self._parent is not None and res == self._parent._tuple:
   162	            return self._parent
   163	        return type(self)(res)
   164	
   165	    def __contains__(self, *args):
   166	        return self._tuple.__contains__(*args)
   167	
   168	    def __ge__(self, *args):
   169	        return self._tuple.__ge__(*args)
   170	
   171	    def __getitem__(self, key):
   172	        tuple_res = self._tuple[key]
   173	        if isinstance(key, slice) and isinstance(tuple_res, tuple):
   174	            tuple_res = type(self)(tuple_res)
   175	            tuple_res._parent = self
   176	        return tuple_res
   177	
   178	    def __gt__(self, *args):
   179	        return self._tuple.__gt__(*args)
   180	
   181	    def __iter__(self, *args):
   182	        return self._tuple.__iter__(*args)
   183	
   184	    def __le__(self, *args):
   185	        return self._tuple.__le__(*args)
   186	
   187	    def __len__(self, *args):
   188	        return self._tuple.__len__(*args)
   189	
   190	    def __lt__(self, *args):
   191	        return self._tuple.__lt__(*args)
   192	
   193	    def __mul__(self, *args):
   194	        return type(self)(self._tuple.__mul__(*args))
   195	
   196	    def __rmul__(self, *args):
   197	        return type(self)(self._tuple.__rmul__(*args))
   198	
   199	    def __radd__(self, x):
   200	        res = x + self._tuple  # type(self)(x + self._tuple)
   201	        if self._parent is not None and res == self._parent._tuple:
   202	            return self._parent
   203	        return type(self)(res)
   204	
   205	    def __str__(self):
   206	        return f"e({', '.join(tuple(str(i) for i in self._tuple))})"
   207	
   208	    def __repr__(self):
   209	        return f"ExpressionTuple({etuple_repr.repr(self._tuple)})"
   210	
   211	    def _repr_pretty_(self, p, cycle):
   212	        if cycle:
   213	            p.text(f"e(...)")  # pragma: no cover
   214	        else:
   215	            with p.group(2, "e(", ")"):
   216	                p.breakable(sep="")
   217	                for idx, item in enumerate(self._tuple):
   218	                    if idx:
   219	                        p.text(",")
   220	                        p.breakable()
   221	                    p.pretty(item)
   222	
   223	    def __eq__(self, other):
   224	        return self._tuple == other
   225	
   226	    def __hash__(self):
   227	        return hash(self._tuple)
   228	
   229	
   230	def etuple(*args, **kwargs):
   231	    """Create an ExpressionTuple from the argument list.
   232	
   233	    In other words:
   234	        etuple(1, 2, 3) == ExpressionTuple((1, 2, 3))
   235	
   236	    """
   237	    return ExpressionTuple(args, **kwargs)
   238

     1	from collections.abc import Callable, Sequence, Mapping
     2	
     3	from multipledispatch import dispatch
     4	
     5	from cons.core import ConsError, ConsNull, ConsPair, car, cdr, cons
     6	
     7	from .core import etuple, ExpressionTuple
     8	
     9	try:
    10	    from packaging import version
    11	    import unification
    12	
    13	    if version.parse(unification.__version__) < version.parse("0.4.0"):
    14	        raise ModuleNotFoundError()
    15	
    16	    from unification.core import _reify, _unify, isvar, construction_sentinel
    17	except ModuleNotFoundError:
    18	    pass
    19	else:
    20	
    21	    def _unify_ExpressionTuple(u, v, s):
    22	        yield _unify(getattr(u, "_tuple", u), getattr(v, "_tuple", v), s)
    23	
    24	    _unify.add((ExpressionTuple, ExpressionTuple, Mapping), _unify_ExpressionTuple)
    25	    _unify.add((tuple, ExpressionTuple, Mapping), _unify_ExpressionTuple)
    26	    _unify.add((ExpressionTuple, tuple, Mapping), _unify_ExpressionTuple)
    27	
    28	    def _reify_ExpressionTuple(u, s):
    29	        # The point of all this: we don't want to lose the expression
    30	        # tracking/caching information.
    31	        res = yield _reify(u._tuple, s)
    32	
    33	        yield construction_sentinel
    34	
    35	        res_same = tuple(
    36	            a == b for a, b in zip(u, res) if not isvar(a) and not isvar(b)
    37	        )
    38	
    39	        if len(res_same) == len(u) and all(res_same):
    40	            # Everything is equal and there are no logic variables
    41	            yield u
    42	            return
    43	
    44	        if getattr(u, "_parent", None) and all(res_same):
    45	            # If we simply swapped-out logic variables, then we don't want to
    46	            # lose the parent etuple information.
    47	            res = etuple(*res)
    48	            res._parent = u._parent
    49	            yield res
    50	            return
    51	
    52	        yield etuple(*res)
    53	
    54	    _reify.add((ExpressionTuple, Mapping), _reify_ExpressionTuple)
    55	
    56	
    57	@dispatch(object)
    58	def rator(x):
    59	    return car(x)
    60	
    61	
    62	@dispatch(object)
    63	def rands(x):
    64	    return cdr(x)
    65	
    66	
    67	@dispatch(object, Sequence)
    68	def apply(rator, rands):
    69	    res = cons(rator, rands)
    70	    return etuple(*res)
    71	
    72	
    73	@apply.register(Callable, Sequence)
    74	def apply_Sequence(rator, rands):
    75	    return rator(*rands)
    76	
    77	
    78	@apply.register(Callable, ExpressionTuple)
    79	def apply_ExpressionTuple(rator, rands):
    80	    return ((rator,) + rands).eval_obj
    81	
    82	
    83	# These are used to maintain some parity with the old `kanren.term` API
    84	operator, arguments, term = rator, rands, apply
    85	
    86	
    87	@dispatch(object)
    88	def etuplize(x, shallow=False, return_bad_args=False, convert_ConsPairs=True):
    89	    """Return an expression-tuple for an object (i.e. a tuple of rand and rators).
    90	
    91	    When evaluated, the rand and rators should [re-]construct the object.  When
    92	    the object cannot be given such a form, it is simply converted to an
    93	    `ExpressionTuple` and returned.
    94	
    95	    Parameters
    96	    ----------
    97	    x: object
    98	      Object to convert to expression-tuple form.
    99	    shallow: bool
   100	      Whether or not to do a shallow conversion.
   101	    return_bad_args: bool
   102	      Return the passed argument when its type is not appropriate, instead
   103	      of raising an exception.
   104	
   105	    """
   106	    if isinstance(x, ExpressionTuple):
   107	        return x
   108	    elif convert_ConsPairs and x is not None and isinstance(x, (ConsNull, ConsPair)):
   109	        return etuple(*x)
   110	
   111	    try:
   112	        op, args = rator(x), rands(x)
   113	    except ConsError:
   114	        op, args = None, None
   115	
   116	    if not callable(op) or not isinstance(args, (ConsNull, ConsPair)):
   117	        if return_bad_args:
   118	            return x
   119	        else:
   120	            raise TypeError(f"x is neither a non-str Sequence nor term: {type(x)}")
   121	
   122	    if shallow:
   123	        et_op = op
   124	        et_args = args
   125	    else:
   126	        et_op = etuplize(op, return_bad_args=True)
   127	        et_args = tuple(
   128	            etuplize(a, return_bad_args=True, convert_ConsPairs=False) for a in args
   129	        )
   130	
   131	    res = etuple(et_op, *et_args, eval_obj=x)
   132	    return res
   133

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me examine the recursive nature of both functions more carefully:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the code, I can see the recursive patterns:

1. In `ExpressionTuple.eval_obj` (lines 113-153 in core.py):
   - Line 127: `op = getattr(op, "eval_obj", op)` - This can recursively call eval_obj on the operator
   - Line 132: `evaled_args = [getattr(i, "eval_obj", i) for i in self._tuple[1:]]` - This recursively calls eval_obj on each argument

2. In `etuplize` (lines 88-132 in dispatch.py):
   - Line 126: `et_op = etuplize(op, return_bad_args=True)` - Recursive call on operator  
   - Line 128: `etuplize(a, return_bad_args=True, convert_ConsPairs=False) for a in args` - Recursive calls on arguments

Both functions can cause deep recursion when dealing with deeply nested expressions, leading to potential stack overflow issues.

The solution needs to implement trampolining to avoid deep recursion. Looking at the referenced unification PR, I should implement a trampoline mechanism that converts recursive calls into iterative processing.

Let me understand what needs to be changed:
1. For `eval_obj`: Instead of recursively calling eval_obj, we need to return continuation objects that can be processed iteratively
2. For `etuplize`: Instead of recursively calling etuplize, we need a similar trampoline approach

The basic idea of trampolining is to:
1. Convert recursive calls into objects representing the computation to be done
2. Process these computations in a loop until we get a final result
3. This avoids growing the call stack

Let me look at existing examples of trampolining patterns to understand the approach better.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me check if there are any existing trampolining utilities or examples I can reference:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2 && python -c "import toolz; print(dir(toolz))"</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>['__builtins__', '__cached__', '__doc__', '__file__', '__loader__', '__name__', '__package__', '__path__', '__spec__', '__version__', '_signatures', '_version', 'accumulate', 'apply', 'assoc', 'assoc_in', 'comp', 'complement', 'compose', 'compose_left', 'concat', 'concatv', 'cons', 'count', 'countby', 'curried', 'curry', 'dicttoolz', 'diff', 'dissoc', 'do', 'drop', 'excepts', 'filter', 'first', 'flip', 'frequencies', 'functoolz', 'get', 'get_in', 'groupby', 'identity', 'interleave', 'interpose', 'isdistinct', 'isiterable', 'itemfilter', 'itemmap', 'iterate', 'itertoolz', 'join', 'juxt', 'keyfilter', 'keymap', 'last', 'map', 'mapcat', 'memoize', 'merge', 'merge_sorted', 'merge_with', 'nth', 'partial', 'partition', 'partition_all', 'partitionby', 'peek', 'peekn', 'pipe', 'pluck', 'random_sample', 'recipes', 'reduce', 'reduceby', 'remove', 'sandbox', 'second', 'sliding_window', 'sorted', 'tail', 'take', 'take_nth', 'thread_first', 'thread_last', 'topk', 'unique', 'update_in', 'utils', 'valfilter', 'valmap']

Let me first create a script to reproduce the recursion issue:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/test_recursion_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Script to reproduce the recursion issue with deeply nested expressions.
"""

import sys
import traceback
from etuples import etuple

def test_deep_eval_obj_recursion():
    """Test that demonstrates deep recursion in eval_obj"""
    print("Testing deep eval_obj recursion...")
    
    # Create a deeply nested expression
    # We'll build: add(add(add(...add(1, 1)...), 1), 1) with many levels
    depth = 1000
    
    # Start with the innermost expression
    expr = etuple(lambda x, y: x + y, 1, 1)
    
    # Build nested expressions
    for i in range(depth - 1):
        expr = etuple(lambda x, y: x + y, expr, 1)
        
    print(f"Created expression with depth {depth}")
    print(f"Expression structure: {expr[:3]}...")  # Show first few elements
    
    try:
        # This should cause recursion error with current implementation
        result = expr.eval_obj
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print("SUCCESS: No recursion error occurred")
        return True
    except RecursionError as e:
        print(f"RECURSION ERROR: {e}")
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"OTHER ERROR: {e}")
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

def test_deep_etuplize_recursion():
    """Test that demonstrates deep recursion in etuplize"""
    print("\nTesting deep etuplize recursion...")
    
    # Create a deeply nested structure that would require deep etuplizing
    class Node:
        def __init__(self, op, *args):
            self.op = op
            self.args = args
            
        def __eq__(self, other):
            return isinstance(other, Node) and self.op == other.op and self.args == other.args
    
    # Mock rator/rands for Node
    from etuples.dispatch import rator, rands
    from etuples.dispatch import apply as dispatch_apply
    
    @rator.register(Node)
    def rator_Node(x):
        return x.op
    
    @rands.register(Node)  
    def rands_Node(x):
        return x.args
    
    # Create deeply nested Node structure
    depth = 1000
    
    # Build from inside out
    node = Node(lambda x, y: x + y, 1, 1)
    
    for i in range(depth - 1):
        node = Node(lambda x, y: x + y, node, 1)
        
    print(f"Created Node structure with depth {depth}")
    
    try:
        from etuples.dispatch import etuplize
        # This should cause recursion error with current implementation
        result = etuplize(node)
        print(f"Etuple result created: {type(result)}")
        print("SUCCESS: No recursion error occurred")
        return True
    except RecursionError as e:
        print(f"RECURSION ERROR: {e}")
        #traceback.print_exc()
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"OTHER ERROR: {e}")
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Testing recursion issues in etuples...")
    
    success1 = test_deep_eval_obj_recursion()
    success2 = test_deep_etuplize_recursion()
    
    if not success1 or not success2:
        print("\nOne or more recursion issues detected - this confirms the problem exists.")
        sys.exit(1)
    else:
        print("\nNo recursion issues detected - either the problem is fixed or not severe enough.")

"""
Simple script to reproduce the recursion issue with deeply nested expressions.
"""

import sys
import traceback
from etuples import etuple

def test_deep_eval_obj_recursion():
    """Test that demonstrates deep recursion in eval_obj"""
    print("Testing deep eval_obj recursion...")
    
    # Create a deeply nested expression
    # We'll build: add(add(add(...add(1, 1)...), 1), 1) with fewer levels to avoid str() issues
    depth = 500  # Increased to trigger recursion
    
    # Start with the innermost expression
    expr = etuple(lambda x, y: x + y, 1, 1)
    
    # Build nested expressions
    for i in range(depth - 1):
        expr = etuple(lambda x, y: x + y, expr, 1)
        
    print(f"Created expression with depth {depth}")
    
    try:
        # This should cause recursion error with current implementation
        result = expr.eval_obj
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print("SUCCESS: No recursion error occurred")
        return True
    except RecursionError as e:
        print(f"RECURSION ERROR: {e}")
        # Don't print full traceback to keep output clean
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"OTHER ERROR: {e}")
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

def test_deep_etuplize_recursion():
    """Test that demonstrates deep recursion in etuplize"""
    print("\nTesting deep etuplize recursion...")
    
    # Create a deeply nested structure that would require deep etuplizing
    class Node:
        def __init__(self, op, *args):
            self.op = op
            self.args = args
            
        def __eq__(self, other):
            return isinstance(other, Node) and self.op == other.op and self.args == other.args
    
    # Mock rator/rands for Node
    from etuples.dispatch import rator, rands
    
    @rator.register(Node)
    def rator_Node(x):
        return x.op
    
    @rands.register(Node)  
    def rands_Node(x):
        return x.args
    
    # Create deeply nested Node structure
    depth = 500
    
    # Build from inside out
    node = Node(lambda x, y: x + y, 1, 1)
    
    for i in range(depth - 1):
        node = Node(lambda x, y: x + y, node, 1)
        
    print(f"Created Node structure with depth {depth}")
    
    try:
        from etuples.dispatch import etuplize
        # This should cause recursion error with current implementation
        result = etuplize(node)
        print(f"Etuple result created: {type(result)}")
        print("SUCCESS: No recursion error occurred")
        return True
    except RecursionError as e:
        print(f"RECURSION ERROR: {e}")
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"OTHER ERROR: {e}")
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Testing recursion issues in etuples...")
    
    success1 = test_deep_eval_obj_recursion()
    success2 = test_deep_etuplize_recursion()
    
    if not success1 or not success2:
        print("\nOne or more recursion issues confirmed.")
        sys.exit(1)
    else:
        print("\nNo recursion issues detected.")

## Phase 5: FIX ANALYSIS - Understanding the Problem and Solution<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I understand the problem clearly:

**Problem Analysis:**

1. **ExpressionTuple.eval_obj recursion**: 
   - Located in `/workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/etuples/core.py`, lines 113-153
   - Recursive calls happen on lines 127 (`op = getattr(op, "eval_obj", op)`) and 132 (`evaled_args = [getattr(i, "eval_obj", i) for i in self._tuple[1:]]`)
   - When evaluating deeply nested expressions, each level makes recursive calls to eval_obj, eventually exceeding the stack limit

2. **etuplize recursion**:
   - Located in `/workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/etuples/dispatch.py`, lines 88-132
   - Recursive calls happen on lines 126 (`et_op = etuplize(op, return_bad_args=True)`) and 128 (`etuplize(a, return_bad_args=True, convert_ConsPairs=False) for a in args`)
   - When converting deeply nested structures, each level makes recursive calls to etuplize, eventually exceeding the stack limit

**Solution Approach - Trampolining:**

Trampolining is a technique to convert recursive algorithms into iterative ones by:
1. Converting recursive calls into objects that represent the computation to be done (continuations)
2. Processing these continuations in a loop until we get a final result
3. This prevents stack growth since we're using heap memory instead

Based on the referenced unification library PR, I need to:

1. **For eval_obj**: Create a trampoline system that processes evaluations iteratively
2. **For etuplize**: Create a trampoline system that processes conversions iteratively

**Implementation Plan:**

I'll need to:
1. Create a trampoline utility (possibly in core.py or a separate utils module)
2. Modify eval_obj to use trampolining instead of direct recursion
3. Modify etuplize to use trampolining instead of direct recursion
4. Ensure backward compatibility - all existing behavior should remain the same

**Best Practices:**
- Minimal changes to preserve existing API
- Preserve caching behavior
- Ensure performance is maintained or improved
- Keep the code readable and maintainable
- Test thoroughly to ensure no regressions

"</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>def stream_eval(z, res_filter=None):
    r"""Evaluate a stream of `_reify`/`_unify` results.

    This implementation consists of a deque that simulates an evaluation stack
    of `_reify`/`_unify`-produced generators.  We're able to overcome
    `RecursionError`\s this way.
    """

    if not isinstance(z, Generator):
        return z

    stack = deque()
    z_args, z_out = None, None
    stack.append(z)

    while stack:
        z = stack[-1]
        try:
            z_out = z.send(z_args)

            if res_filter:
                _ = res_filter(z, z_out)

            if isinstance(z_out, Generator):
                stack.append(z_out)
                z_args = None
            else:
                z_args = z_out

        except StopIteration:
            _ = stack.pop()

    return z_out

"""
Understanding the trampolining pattern from unification library.
"""

# Based on what I saw, the pattern seems to be:
# 1. Functions return generators instead of doing direct recursion
# 2. Generators yield either intermediate values or other generators
# 3. Special sentinel values indicate construction steps
# 4. A trampoline evaluator processes the generators iteratively

# Let me create a simple example to understand this:

def simple_recursive_func(n):
    """A simple recursive function that could cause stack overflow"""
    if n <= 0:
        return 1
    return n + simple_recursive_func(n - 1)

# Trampolined version:
class Continue:
    def __init__(self, func, *args, **kwargs):
        self.func = func
        self.args = args
        self.kwargs = kwargs

def trampolined_sum(n, acc=0):
    """Trampolined version that returns either result or continuation"""
    if n <= 0:
        return acc
    else:
        # Return a continuation instead of recursing directly
        return Continue(trampolined_sum, n - 1, acc + n)

def trampoline(func, *args, **kwargs):
    """Trampoline evaluator that processes continuations"""
    result = func(*args, **kwargs)
    while isinstance(result, Continue):
        result = result.func(*result.args, **result.kwargs)
    return result

# Test both versions:
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Normal recursion:")
    print(simple_recursive_func(10))
    
    print("Trampolined recursion:")
    print(trampoline(trampolined_sum, 10))
    
    # Test with larger numbers that would cause stack overflow normally
    try:
        print("Normal recursion with large number:")
        print(simple_recursive_func(1000))  # This might work depending on system
    except RecursionError:
        print("RecursionError caught")
    
    print("Trampolined recursion with large number:")
    print(trampoline(trampolined_sum, 1000))</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/understand_trampolining.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2 && python understand_trampolining.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Normal recursion:

I'll create a helper function that returns continuations:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/etuples/core.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _eval_obj_trampoline(self):
        """Trampolined version of eval_obj evaluation."""
        if len(self._tuple) == 0:
            raise InvalidExpression()

        if self._eval_obj is not self.null:
            return self._eval_obj
        else:
            op = self._tuple[0]
            # Instead of recursively calling getattr(..., "eval_obj", ...),
            # we return a continuation if needed
            if hasattr(op, "eval_obj"):
                op_eval = op.eval_obj
                if isinstance(op_eval, _Continue):
                    # If op.eval_obj returned a continuation, we need to handle it
                    return _Continue(lambda: self._eval_obj_trampoline_after_op(op_eval, self._tuple[1:]))
            
            op = getattr(op, "eval_obj", op)

            if not callable(op):
                raise InvalidExpression()

            # Process arguments with trampolining
            return self._eval_args_and_apply(op, self._tuple[1:])
    
    def _eval_obj_trampoline_after_op(self, op_result, remaining_args):
        """Continue evaluation after getting operator result."""
        op = op_result
        if not callable(op):
            raise InvalidExpression()
        return self._eval_args_and_apply(op, remaining_args)
    
    def _eval_args_and_apply(self, op, args):
        """Evaluate arguments and apply operator with trampolining."""
        evaled_args = []
        evaled_kwargs = []
        
        # Process positional arguments first
        for i, arg in enumerate(args):
            if isinstance(arg, KwdPair):
                # Handle keyword arguments later
                evaled_kwargs.extend(args[i:])
                break
            else:
                evaled_arg = getattr(arg, "eval_obj", arg)
                if isinstance(evaled_arg, _Continue):
                    # Need to continue processing after this argument
                    return _Continue(
                        lambda evaluated_arg=evaled_arg, processed_args=evaled_args[:], 
                               remaining_args=args[i+1:]: 
                            self._continue_eval_args(evaluated_arg, processed_args, remaining_args, op)
                    )
                evaled_args.append(evaled_arg)
        
        # Group arguments by type
        arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), evaled_args + evaled_kwargs)
        final_evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
        final_evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])
        
        # Apply the operation
        try:
            op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
        except ValueError:
            # This handles some builtin function types
            _eval_obj = op(*(final_evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs]))
        else:
            op_args = op_sig.bind(
                *final_evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs}
            )
            op_args.apply_defaults()
            _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)

        self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
        return self._eval_obj
    
    def _continue_eval_args(self, current_arg_result, processed_args, remaining_args, op):
        """Continue processing arguments after evaluating one."""
        processed_args.append(current_arg_result)
        
        # Process remaining arguments
        for i, arg in enumerate(remaining_args):
            if isinstance(arg, KwdPair):
                # Handle remaining as keyword arguments
                evaled_remaining = []
                for rem_arg in remaining_args[i:]:
                    evaled_rem = getattr(rem_arg, "eval_obj", rem_arg)
                    if isinstance(evaled_rem, _Continue):
                        return _Continue(
                            lambda evaluated_rem=evaled_rem, proc_args=processed_args[:], 
                                   rem_args=remaining_args[i+1:], r_idx=i:
                                self._continue_eval_args(evaluated_rem, proc_args, 
                                                       list(rem_args), op)
                        )
                    evaled_remaining.append(evaled_rem)
                
                # Combine all arguments and apply
                all_args = processed_args + evaled_remaining
                arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), all_args)
                final_evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
                final_evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])
                
                try:
                    op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
                except ValueError:
                    _eval_obj = op(*(final_evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs]))
                else:
                    op_args = op_sig.bind(
                        *final_evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs}
                    )
                    op_args.apply_defaults()
                    _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)
                
                self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
                return self._eval_obj
            
            else:
                evaled_arg = getattr(arg, "eval_obj", arg)
                if isinstance(evaled_arg, _Continue):
                    return _Continue(
                        lambda evaluated_arg=evaled_arg, proc_args=processed_args[:], 
                               rem_args=remaining_args[i+1:]:
                            self._continue_eval_args(evaluated_arg, proc_args, rem_args, op)
                    )
                processed_args.append(evaled_arg)
        
        # All arguments processed, apply operation
        arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), processed_args)
        final_evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
        final_evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])
        
        try:
            op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
        except ValueError:
            _eval_obj = op(*(final_evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs]))
        else:
            op_args = op_sig.bind(
                *final_evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs}
            )
            op_args.apply_defaults()
            _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)
        
        self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
        return self._eval_obj

    @property
    def eval_obj(self):
        """Return the evaluation of this expression tuple.

        Warning: If the evaluation value isn't cached, it will be evaluated
        recursively.

        """
        return _trampoline(self._eval_obj_trampoline)</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    @property
    def eval_obj(self):
        """Return the evaluation of this expression tuple.

        Warning: If the evaluation value isn't cached, it will be evaluated
        recursively.

        """
        if len(self._tuple) == 0:
            raise InvalidExpression()

        if self._eval_obj is not self.null:
            return self._eval_obj
        else:
            op = self._tuple[0]
            op = getattr(op, "eval_obj", op)

            if not callable(op):
                raise InvalidExpression()

            evaled_args = [getattr(i, "eval_obj", i) for i in self._tuple[1:]]
            arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), evaled_args)
            evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
            evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])

            try:
                op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
            except ValueError:
                # This handles some builtin function types
                _eval_obj = op(*(evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in evaled_kwargs]))
            else:
                op_args = op_sig.bind(
                    *evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in evaled_kwargs}
                )
                op_args.apply_defaults()

                _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)

            # assert not isinstance(_eval_obj, ExpressionTuple)

            self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
            return self._eval_obj</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/etuples/core.py has been edited.    159	        return self._eval_args_and_apply(op, remaining_args)
   160	    
   161	    def _eval_args_and_apply(self, op, args):
   162	        """Evaluate arguments and apply operator with trampolining."""
   163	        evaled_args = []
   164	        evaled_kwargs = []
   165	        
   166	        # Process positional arguments first
   167	        for i, arg in enumerate(args):
   168	            if isinstance(arg, KwdPair):
   169	                # Handle keyword arguments later
   170	                evaled_kwargs.extend(args[i:])
   171	                break
   172	            else:
   173	                evaled_arg = getattr(arg, "eval_obj", arg)
   174	                if isinstance(evaled_arg, _Continue):
   175	                    # Need to continue processing after this argument
   176	                    return _Continue(
   177	                        lambda evaluated_arg=evaled_arg, processed_args=evaled_args[:], 
   178	                               remaining_args=args[i+1:]: 
   179	                            self._continue_eval_args(evaluated_arg, processed_args, remaining_args, op)
   180	                    )
   181	                evaled_args.append(evaled_arg)
   182	        
   183	        # Group arguments by type
   184	        arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), evaled_args + evaled_kwargs)
   185	        final_evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
   186	        final_evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])
   187	        
   188	        # Apply the operation
   189	        try:
   190	            op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
   191	        except ValueError:
   192	            # This handles some builtin function types
   193	            _eval_obj = op(*(final_evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs]))
   194	        else:
   195	            op_args = op_sig.bind(
   196	                *final_evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs}
   197	            )
   198	            op_args.apply_defaults()
   199	            _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)
   200	
   201	        self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
   202	        return self._eval_obj
   203	    
   204	    def _continue_eval_args(self, current_arg_result, processed_args, remaining_args, op):
   205	        """Continue processing arguments after evaluating one."""
   206	        processed_args.append(current_arg_result)
   207	        
   208	        # Process remaining arguments
   209	        for i, arg in enumerate(remaining_args):
   210	            if isinstance(arg, KwdPair):
   211	                # Handle remaining as keyword arguments
   212	                evaled_remaining = []
   213	                for rem_arg in remaining_args[i:]:
   214	                    evaled_rem = getattr(rem_arg, "eval_obj", rem_arg)
   215	                    if isinstance(evaled_rem, _Continue):
   216	                        return _Continue(
   217	                            lambda evaluated_rem=evaled_rem, proc_args=processed_args[:], 
   218	                                   rem_args=remaining_args[i+1:], r_idx=i:
   219	                                self._continue_eval_args(evaluated_rem, proc_args, 
   220	                                                       list(rem_args), op)
   221	                        )
   222	                    evaled_remaining.append(evaled_rem)
   223	                
   224	                # Combine all arguments and apply
   225	                all_args = processed_args + evaled_remaining
   226	                arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), all_args)
   227	                final_evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
   228	                final_evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])
   229	                
   230	                try:
   231	                    op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
   232	                except ValueError:
   233	                    _eval_obj = op(*(final_evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs]))
   234	                else:
   235	                    op_args = op_sig.bind(
   236	                        *final_evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs}
   237	                    )
   238	                    op_args.apply_defaults()
   239	                    _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)
   240	                
   241	                self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
   242	                return self._eval_obj
   243	            
   244	            else:
   245	                evaled_arg = getattr(arg, "eval_obj", arg)
   246	                if isinstance(evaled_arg, _Continue):
   247	                    return _Continue(
   248	                        lambda evaluated_arg=evaled_arg, proc_args=processed_args[:], 
   249	                               rem_args=remaining_args[i+1:]:
   250	                            self._continue_eval_args(evaluated_arg, proc_args, rem_args, op)
   251	                    )
   252	                processed_args.append(evaled_arg)
   253	        
   254	        # All arguments processed, apply operation
   255	        arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), processed_args)
   256	        final_evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
   257	        final_evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])
   258	        
   259	        try:
   260	            op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
   261	        except ValueError:
   262	            _eval_obj = op(*(final_evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs]))
   263	        else:
   264	            op_args = op_sig.bind(
   265	                *final_evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs}
   266	            )
   267	            op_args.apply_defaults()
   268	            _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)
   269	        
   270	        self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
   271	        return self._eval_obj
   272	
   273	    @property
   274	    def eval_obj(self):
   275	        """Return the evaluation of this expression tuple.
   276	
   277	        Warning: If the evaluation value isn't cached, it will be evaluated
   278	        recursively.
   279	
   280	        """
   281	        return _trampoline(self._eval_obj_trampoline)
   282	
   283	    @eval_obj.setter
   284	    def eval_obj(self, obj):
   285	        raise ValueError("Value of evaluated expression cannot be set!")

class _Continue:
    """A continuation object for trampolining."""
    def __init__(self, func, *args, **kwargs):
        self.func = func
        self.args = args
        self.kwargs = kwargs


def _trampoline(func, *args, **kwargs):
    """Trampoline function that evaluates continuations iteratively."""
    result = func(*args, **kwargs)
    while isinstance(result, _Continue):
        result = result.func(*result.args, **result.kwargs)

# Let me understand the pattern by creating a simpler trampoline using deque like unification does
from collections import deque

class EvalTask:
    '''Represents a task to evaluate an expression'''
    def __init__(self, expr_tuple):
        self.expr_tuple = expr_tuple

def trampoline_eval(initial_expr):
    '''Iterative evaluation using deque as stack'''
    stack = deque([EvalTask(initial_expr)])
    results = {}
    
    while stack:
        task = stack[-1]
        
        # If we've already computed this, use cached result
        if id(task.expr_tuple) in results:
            stack.pop()
            continue
            
        # Check if already cached in the expression itself
        if task.expr_tuple._eval_obj is not task.expr_tuple.null:
            results[id(task.expr_tuple)] = task.expr_tuple._eval_obj
            stack.pop()
            continue
        
        # Get operator
        op = task.expr_tuple._tuple[0]
        if hasattr(op, 'eval_obj'):
            if id(op) not in results and op._eval_obj is op.null:
                # Need to evaluate operator first
                stack.append(EvalTask(op))
                continue
            else:
                op = getattr(op, 'eval_obj', op)
        
        # Check if operator is callable
        if not callable(op):
            raise Exception('Invalid operator')
        
        # Process arguments
        args_ready = True
        evaled_args = []
        
        for arg in task.expr_tuple._tuple[1:]:
            if hasattr(arg, 'eval_obj'):
                if id(arg) not in results and arg._eval_obj is arg.null:
                    # Need to evaluate argument first
                    stack.append(EvalTask(arg))
                    args_ready = False
                    break
                else:
                    evaled_args.append(getattr(arg, 'eval_obj', arg))
            else:
                evaled_args.append(arg)
        
        if not args_ready:
            continue
            
        # All ready, compute result
        try:
            _eval_obj = op(*evaled_args)
        except Exception as e:
            stack.pop()
            raise e
            
        task.expr_tuple._eval_obj = _eval_obj
        results[id(task.expr_tuple)] = _eval_obj
        stack.pop()
    
    return results[id(initial_expr)]

# Let me understand the pattern by creating a simpler trampoline using deque like unification does
from collections import deque
class EvalTask:
    '''Represents a task to evaluate an expression'''
    def __init__(self, expr_tuple):
        self.expr_tuple = expr_tuple
def trampoline_eval(initial_expr):
    '''Iterative evaluation using deque as stack'''
    stack = deque([EvalTask(initial_expr)])
    results = {}

    while stack:
        task = stack[-1]

        # If we've already computed this, use cached result
        if id(task.expr_tuple) in results:
            stack.pop()
            continue

        # Check if already cached in the expression itself
        if task.expr_tuple._eval_obj is not task.expr_tuple.null:
            results[id(task.expr_tuple)] = task.expr_tuple._eval_obj
            stack.pop()
            continue

        # Get operator
        op = task.expr_tuple._tuple[0]
        if hasattr(op, 'eval_obj'):
            if id(op) not in results and op._eval_obj is op.null:
                # Need to evaluate operator first
                stack.append(EvalTask(op))
                continue
            else:
                op = getattr(op, 'eval_obj', op)

        # Check if operator is callable
        if not callable(op):
            raise Exception('Invalid operator')

        # Process arguments
        args_ready = True
        evaled_args = []

        for arg in task.expr_tuple._tuple[1:]:
            if hasattr(arg, 'eval_obj'):
                if id(arg) not in results and arg._eval_obj is arg.null:
                    # Need to evaluate argument first
                    stack.append(EvalTask(arg))
                    args_ready = False
                    break
                else:
                    evaled_args.append(getattr(arg, 'eval_obj', arg))
            else:
                evaled_args.append(arg)

        if not args_ready:
            continue

        # All ready, compute result
        try:
            _eval_obj = op(*evaled_args)
        except Exception as e:
            stack.pop()
            raise e

        task.expr_tuple._eval_obj = _eval_obj
        results[id(task.expr_tuple)] = _eval_obj
        stack.pop()

    return results[id(initial_expr)]

class ExpressionTuple(Sequence):
    """A tuple-like object that represents an expression.

    This object caches the return value resulting from evaluation of the
    expression it represents.  Likewise, it holds onto the "parent" expression
    from which it was derived (e.g. as a slice), if any, so that it can
    preserve the return value through limited forms of concatenation/cons-ing
    that would reproduce the parent expression.

    TODO: Should probably use weakrefs for that.
    """

    __slots__ = ("_eval_obj", "_tuple", "_parent")
    null = object()

    def __new__(cls, seq=None, **kwargs):

        # XXX: This doesn't actually remove the entry from the kwargs
        # passed to __init__!
        # It does, however, remove it for the check below.
        kwargs.pop("eval_obj", None)

        if seq is not None and not kwargs and type(seq) == cls:
            return seq

        res = super().__new__(cls)

    85	
    86	
    87	@dispatch(object)
    88	def etuplize(x, shallow=False, return_bad_args=False, convert_ConsPairs=True):
    89	    """Return an expression-tuple for an object (i.e. a tuple of rand and rators).
    90	
    91	    When evaluated, the rand and rators should [re-]construct the object.  When
    92	    the object cannot be given such a form, it is simply converted to an
    93	    `ExpressionTuple` and returned.
    94	
    95	    Parameters
    96	    ----------
    97	    x: object
    98	      Object to convert to expression-tuple form.
    99	    shallow: bool
   100	      Whether or not to do a shallow conversion.
   101	    return_bad_args: bool
   102	      Return the passed argument when its type is not appropriate, instead
   103	      of raising an exception.
   104	
   105	    """
   106	    if isinstance(x, ExpressionTuple):
   107	        return x
   108	    elif convert_ConsPairs and x is not None and isinstance(x, (ConsNull, ConsPair)):
   109	        return etuple(*x)
   110	
   111	    try:
   112	        op, args = rator(x), rands(x)
   113	    except ConsError:
   114	        op, args = None, None
   115	
   116	    if not callable(op) or not isinstance(args, (ConsNull, ConsPair)):
   117	        if return_bad_args:
   118	            return x
   119	        else:
   120	            raise TypeError(f"x is neither a non-str Sequence nor term: {type(x)}")
   121	
   122	    if shallow:
   123	        et_op = op
   124	        et_args = args
   125	    else:
   126	        et_op = etuplize(op, return_bad_args=True)
   127	        et_args = tuple(
   128	            etuplize(a, return_bad_args=True, convert_ConsPairs=False) for a in args
   129	        )
   130	
   131	    res = etuple(et_op, *et_args, eval_obj=x)
   132	    return res

import reprlib
from collections import deque

import toolz

from collections.abc import Sequence


class _TrampolineSentinel:
    """Sentinel object for trampolining."""
    pass


_construction_sentinel = _TrampolineSentinel()


def _trampoline_eval(gen):
    """Evaluate a generator-based trampolined computation."""
    if not isinstance(gen, type(_trampoline_eval).__code__.__class__.__bases__[0]):  # Not a generator
        return gen

    stack = deque()
    z_args, z_out = None, None
    stack.append(gen)

    while stack:
        z = stack[-1]
        try:
            z_out = z.send(z_args)

            if isinstance(z_out, type(_trampoline_eval).__code__.__bases__[0]):  # Generator
                stack.append(z_out)
                z_args = None
            else:
                z_args = z_out

        except StopIteration as e:
            stack.pop()
            z_out = e.value

    return z_out</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>import inspect

I'll create a generator-based approach:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/etuples/core.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _eval_obj_generator(self):
        """Generator-based evaluation for trampolining."""
        if len(self._tuple) == 0:
            raise InvalidExpression()

        if self._eval_obj is not self.null:
            yield self._eval_obj
            return

        op = self._tuple[0]
        # Handle operator evaluation
        if hasattr(op, "eval_obj"):
            op_result = op.eval_obj
            # If it's a generator (from trampolining), evaluate it
            if isinstance(op_result, GeneratorType):
                op = yield op_result
            else:
                op = op_result
        else:
            op = getattr(op, "eval_obj", op)

        if not callable(op):
            raise InvalidExpression()

        # Evaluate arguments
        evaled_args = []
        for i, arg in enumerate(self._tuple[1:]):
            if hasattr(arg, "eval_obj"):
                arg_result = arg.eval_obj
                # If it's a generator (from trampolining), evaluate it
                if isinstance(arg_result, GeneratorType):
                    evaled_arg = yield arg_result
                else:
                    evaled_arg = arg_result
            else:
                evaled_arg = arg
            evaled_args.append(evaled_arg)

        # Group arguments by type
        arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), evaled_args)
        final_evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
        final_evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])

        # Apply the operation
        try:
            op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
        except ValueError:
            # This handles some builtin function types
            _eval_obj = op(*(final_evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs]))
        else:
            op_args = op_sig.bind(
                *final_evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs}
            )
            op_args.apply_defaults()
            _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)

        self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
        yield _eval_obj

    @property
    def eval_obj(self):
        """Return the evaluation of this expression tuple.

        Warning: If the evaluation value isn't cached, it will be evaluated
        recursively.

        """
        return _trampoline_eval(self._eval_obj_generator())</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    @property
    def eval_obj(self):
        """Return the evaluation of this expression tuple.

        Warning: If the evaluation value isn't cached, it will be evaluated
        recursively.

        """
        if len(self._tuple) == 0:
            raise InvalidExpression()

        if self._eval_obj is not self.null:
            return self._eval_obj
        else:
            op = self._tuple[0]
            op = getattr(op, "eval_obj", op)

            if not callable(op):
                raise InvalidExpression()

            evaled_args = [getattr(i, "eval_obj", i) for i in self._tuple[1:]]
            arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), evaled_args)
            evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
            evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])

            try:
                op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
            except ValueError:
                # This handles some builtin function types
                _eval_obj = op(*(evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in evaled_kwargs]))
            else:
                op_args = op_sig.bind(
                    *evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in evaled_kwargs}
                )
                op_args.apply_defaults()

                _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)

            # assert not isinstance(_eval_obj, ExpressionTuple)

            self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
            return self._eval_obj</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/etuples/core.py has been edited. Review the changes and make sure they are as expected. Edit the file again if necessary.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now let me create a trampolined version of etuplize. I'll create a generator-based version:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/etuples/dispatch.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _etuplize_generator(x, shallow=False, return_bad_args=False, convert_ConsPairs=True):
    """Generator-based etuplize for trampolining."""
    if isinstance(x, ExpressionTuple):
        yield x
        return
    elif convert_ConsPairs and x is not None and isinstance(x, (ConsNull, ConsPair)):
        yield etuple(*x)
        return

    try:
        op, args = rator(x), rands(x)
    except ConsError:
        op, args = None, None

    if not callable(op) or not isinstance(args, (ConsNull, ConsPair)):
        if return_bad_args:
            yield x
            return
        else:
            raise TypeError(f"x is neither a non-str Sequence nor term: {type(x)}")

    if shallow:
        et_op = op
        et_args = args
    else:
        # Handle operator etuplization
        op_result = etuplize(op, return_bad_args=True)
        if isinstance(op_result, GeneratorType):
            et_op = yield op_result
        else:
            et_op = op_result
            
        # Handle arguments etuplization
        et_args_list = []
        for a in args:
            arg_result = etuplize(a, return_bad_args=True, convert_ConsPairs=False)
            if isinstance(arg_result, GeneratorType):
                et_arg = yield arg_result
            else:
                et_arg = arg_result
            et_args_list.append(et_arg)
        et_args = tuple(et_args_list)

    res = etuple(et_op, *et_args, eval_obj=x)
    yield res


@dispatch(object)
def etuplize(x, shallow=False, return_bad_args=False, convert_ConsPairs=True):
    """Return an expression-tuple for an object (i.e. a tuple of rand and rators).

    When evaluated, the rand and rators should [re-]construct the object.  When
    the object cannot be given such a form, it is simply converted to an
    `ExpressionTuple` and returned.

    Parameters
    ----------
    x: object
      Object to convert to expression-tuple form.
    shallow: bool
      Whether or not to do a shallow conversion.
    return_bad_args: bool
      Return the passed argument when its type is not appropriate, instead
      of raising an exception.

    """
    return _trampoline_eval(_etuplize_generator(x, shallow, return_bad_args, convert_ConsPairs))</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>@dispatch(object)
def etuplize(x, shallow=False, return_bad_args=False, convert_ConsPairs=True):
    """Return an expression-tuple for an object (i.e. a tuple of rand and rators).

    When evaluated, the rand and rators should [re-]construct the object.  When
    the object cannot be given such a form, it is simply converted to an
    `ExpressionTuple` and returned.

    Parameters
    ----------
    x: object
      Object to convert to expression-tuple form.
    shallow: bool
      Whether or not to do a shallow conversion.
    return_bad_args: bool
      Return the passed argument when its type is not appropriate, instead
      of raising an exception.

    """
    if isinstance(x, ExpressionTuple):
        return x
    elif convert_ConsPairs and x is not None and isinstance(x, (ConsNull, ConsPair)):
        return etuple(*x)

    try:
        op, args = rator(x), rands(x)
    except ConsError:
        op, args = None, None

    if not callable(op) or not isinstance(args, (ConsNull, ConsPair)):
        if return_bad_args:
            return x
        else:
            raise TypeError(f"x is neither a non-str Sequence nor term: {type(x)}")

    if shallow:
        et_op = op
        et_args = args
    else:
        et_op = etuplize(op, return_bad_args=True)
        et_args = tuple(
            etuplize(a, return_bad_args=True, convert_ConsPairs=False) for a in args
        )

    res = etuple(et_op, *et_args, eval_obj=x)
    return res</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/etuples/dispatch.py has been edited. def trampoline_eval(gen):
    """Simplified trampoline eval"""
    if not isinstance(gen, GeneratorType):
        return gen

    stack = []
    z_args = None
    stack.append(gen)

    while stack:
        z = stack[-1]
        try:
            if z_args is None:
                z_out = next(z)
            else:
                z_out = z.send(z_args)
            print(f"Got from generator: {z_out}")

            if isinstance(z_out, GeneratorType):
                stack.append(z_out)
                z_args = None
            else:
                z_args = z_out

        except StopIteration as e:
            print(f"Generator finished, popping stack. Value: {e.value}")
            stack.pop()
            z_args = e.value

    return z_args

def gen_that_calls_other_gen():
    print("Main generator started")
    sub_gen = simple_gen()
    result = yield sub_gen
    print(f"Got result from sub-generator: {result}")
    yield f"final: {result}"

    r"""Evaluate a stream of `_reify`/`_unify` results.

    This implementation consists of a deque that simulates an evaluation stack
    of `_reify`/`_unify`-produced generators.  We're able to overcome
    `RecursionError`\s this way.

                stack.append(z_out)
                z_args = None
            else:
                z_args = z_out

        except StopIteration:
            _ = stack.pop()

    return z_out

@_reify.register(Var, Mapping)
def _reify_Var(o, s):
    o_w = walk(o, s)

1. My generator yields another generator
2. The trampoline pushes that generator onto the stack
3. When that sub-generator finishes, its final yielded value should be sent back to my generator
4. My generator receives that value and continues

But I'm not handling the case where my generator yields a final result. Let me create a better test to understand:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/debug_generator2.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

from collections import deque
from types import GeneratorType

def stream_eval(z, debug=False):
    """Copy of the unification stream_eval for testing"""
    if not isinstance(z, GeneratorType):
        return z

    stack = deque()
    z_args, z_out = None, None
    stack.append(z)

    while stack:
        z = stack[-1]
        try:
            if debug:
                print(f"Sending {z_args} to generator {id(z)}")
            if z_args is None:
                z_out = next(z)
            else:
                z_out = z.send(z_args)
            
            if debug:
                print(f"Got {z_out} from generator {id(z)}")

            if isinstance(z_out, GeneratorType):
                if debug:
                    print(f"Pushing new generator {id(z_out)} onto stack")
                stack.append(z_out)
                z_args = None
            else:
                if debug:
                    print(f"Setting z_args to {z_out}")
                z_args = z_out

        except StopIteration as e:
            if debug:
                print(f"Generator {id(z)} finished")
            stack.pop()
            # Note: unification doesn't set z_out = e.value

    if debug:
        print(f"Returning final z_out: {z_out}")
    return z_out

def test_generator():
    """Test generator that yields a final value"""
    print("Generator starting")
    yield "final_result"
    print("Generator ending")

def test_nested_generator():
    """Test generator that yields another generator"""

"""

import sys

def test_deep_eval_obj_recursion():
    """Test that demonstrates deep recursion is now fixed in eval_obj"""
    print("Testing deep eval_obj recursion...")
    
    # Create a deeply nested expression
    # Start with reasonable depth that would cause issues before fix
    depth = 500  
    
    # Start with the innermost expression
    expr = etuple(lambda x, y: x + y, 1, 1)
    
    # Build nested expressions
    for i in range(depth - 1):
        expr = etuple(lambda x, y: x + y, expr, 1)

        # This should now work with trampolining
        result = expr.eval_obj
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print("SUCCESS: No recursion error occurred")
        return True
    except RecursionError as e:
        print(f"RECURSION ERROR: {e}")
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"OTHER ERROR: {e}")
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

"""
Isolate the exact source of recursion.

import sys
import traceback
from etuples import etuple

def test_isolated_eval():
    """Test just the eval_obj call without other operations"""
    print("Creating nested expressions...")
    
    # Turn off any automatic string conversion by avoiding print statements
    depth = 500  
    
    # Start with the innermost expression
    expr = etuple(lambda x, y: x + y, 1, 1)
    
    # Build nested expressions
    for i in range(depth - 1):
        expr = etuple(lambda x, y: x + y, expr, 1)
        
    print(f"Created expression tree with depth {depth}")
    print("Attempting to evaluate...")
    
    try:
        # Only call eval_obj, nothing else
        result = expr.eval_obj
        print(f"Evaluation successful: {result}")
        return True
    except RecursionError as e:
        print("RecursionError during eval_obj")
        # Let's see where exactly
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

        print(f"Other error: {e}")

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I can see the issue! The problem is in my generator implementation. Looking at the traceback:

1. `_trampoline_eval` calls `z.send(z_args)` (line 23)
2. This goes to `_eval_obj_generator` at line 167: `if hasattr(arg, "eval_obj"):`
3. This triggers the `eval_obj` property again (line 207): `return _trampoline_eval(self._eval_obj_generator())`

So the issue is that when I check `hasattr(arg, "eval_obj")`, if `arg` is an ExpressionTuple, accessing `.eval_obj` calls the property which calls `_trampoline_eval` again, creating infinite recursion.

The problem is that I'm not properly intercepting the eval_obj access. I need to access the attribute without triggering the property. Let me fix this:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/etuples/core.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _eval_obj_generator(self):
        """Generator-based evaluation for trampolining."""
        if len(self._tuple) == 0:
            raise InvalidExpression()

        if self._eval_obj is not self.null:
            yield self._eval_obj
            return

        op = self._tuple[0]
        # Handle operator evaluation - check if it has eval_obj without triggering it
        if hasattr(op, 'eval_obj') and not callable(type(op).eval_obj):
            # It's a property, so accessing it will trigger evaluation
            # We need to get the actual eval_obj value
            op_result = op.eval_obj
            # If it's a generator (from trampolining), yield it
            if isinstance(op_result, GeneratorType):
                op = yield op_result
            else:
                op = op_result
        else:
            op = getattr(op, "eval_obj", op)

        if not callable(op):
            raise InvalidExpression()

        # Evaluate arguments
        evaled_args = []
        for i, arg in enumerate(self._tuple[1:]):
            # Check if argument has eval_obj without triggering it if possible
            if hasattr(arg, 'eval_obj') and not callable(type(arg).eval_obj):
                # It's a property
                arg_result = arg.eval_obj
                # If it's a generator (from trampolining), yield it
                if isinstance(arg_result, GeneratorType):
                    evaled_arg = yield arg_result
                else:
                    evaled_arg = arg_result
            else:
                evaled_arg = getattr(arg, "eval_obj", arg)
            evaled_args.append(evaled_arg)

        # Group arguments by type
        arg_grps = toolz.groupby(lambda x: isinstance(x, KwdPair), evaled_args)
        final_evaled_args = arg_grps.get(False, [])
        final_evaled_kwargs = arg_grps.get(True, [])

        # Apply the operation
        try:
            op_sig = inspect.signature(op)
        except ValueError:
            # This handles some builtin function types
            _eval_obj = op(*(final_evaled_args + [kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs]))
        else:
            op_args = op_sig.bind(
                *final_evaled_args, **{kw.arg: kw.value for kw in final_evaled_kwargs}
            )
            op_args.apply_defaults()
            _eval_obj = op(*op_args.args, **op_args.kwargs)

        self._eval_obj = _eval_obj
        yield _eval_obj</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _eval_obj_generator(self):

Actually, let me step back and understand the pattern better. In the unification library, they have `_reify` functions that are generators, and `reify` is a function that calls `stream_eval` on those generators.

But here, I have a property `eval_obj` that should return the evaluated result. Properties can't return generators - they have to return actual values.

So I think the right approach is:
1. Change `eval_obj` from a property to a method, OR
2. Have the property call a trampolined function that returns the final value

But changing the API would break compatibility. Let me stick with approach 2.

The issue in my current implementation is that when I check `hasattr(arg, "eval_obj")` and then access `arg.eval_obj`, if `arg` is an ExpressionTuple, that triggers the property which calls `_trampoline_eval`, which can lead to recursion.

But wait, that shouldn't cause infinite recursion if the trampoline is working correctly. Let me trace through what should happen:

1. Outer tuple calls `eval_obj` -> calls `_trampoline_eval(generator1)`
2. `_trampoline_eval` starts `generator1`
3. `generator1` yields when it encounters nested tuple -> yields `nested_tuple.eval_obj`
4. But `nested_tuple.eval_obj` is also a property that calls `_trampoline_eval(generator2)`
5. This creates nested `_trampoline_eval` calls, causing recursion.

Ah! I see the issue now. The problem is that I'm calling `_trampoline_eval` from within a `_trampoline_eval` call. 

The solution is that when my generator encounters a nested ExpressionTuple, instead of yielding `nested.eval_obj` (which would trigger another trampoline), I should yield the generator from the nested tuple directly.

        if len(self._tuple) == 0:

            yield self._eval_obj
            return

In the unification library, they use `yield` to pass generators up the chain, and the trampoline evaluates them. But I'm using `yield from` which executes the sub-generator immediately.

But I notice that my etuplize function is already using a generator approach, so let me test if it has the same issue:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pythological__etuples__0.2/test_etuplize_recursion.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Test etuplize recursion.
"""

import sys
import traceback

def test_etuplize_recursion():
    """Test that demonstrates deep recursion is now fixed in etuplize"""
    print("Testing deep etuplize recursion...")
    
    # Create a deeply nested structure that would require deep etuplizing
    class Node:
        def __init__(self, op, *args):
            self.op = op
            self.args = args
            
        def __eq__(self, other):
            return isinstance(other, Node) and self.op == other.op and self.args == other.args
    
    # Mock rator/rands for Node
    from etuples.dispatch import rator, rands
    
    @rator.register(Node)
    def rator_Node(x):
        return x.op
    
    @rands.register(Node)  
    def rands_Node(x):
        return x.args
    
    # Create deeply nested Node structure
    depth = 1000
    
    # Build from inside out
    node = Node(lambda x, y: x + y, 1, 1)
    
    for i in range(depth - 1):
        node = Node(lambda x, y: x + y, node, 1)
        
    print(f"Created Node structure with depth {depth}")
    
    try:
        from etuples.dispatch import etuplize
        # This should now work with trampolining
        result = etuplize(node)
        print(f"Etuplize result created: {type(result)}")
        print("SUCCESS: No recursion error occurred")
        return True
    except RecursionError as e:
        print(f"RECURSION ERROR: {e}")
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"OTHER ERROR: {e}")
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Testing that trampolining fixes etuplize recursion...")
    
    success = test_etuplize_recursion()
    
    if success:
        print("\nEtuplize recursion issue is FIXED!")
        sys.exit(0)

        print("\nEtuplize recursion issue still exists.")

# Test simple case first

    def __init__(self, op, args):
        self.op = op
        self.args = args

def rator_SimpleNode(x):
    return x.op

@rands.register(SimpleNode)

But let me look at this more carefully. The issue might be that I need to make the etuplize function properly trampolined by avoiding direct recursive calls.

Looking at my current implementation, I am calling `etuplize(op, return_bad_args=True)` and `etuplize(a, return_bad_args=True, convert_ConsPairs=False)` directly within the generator. But what I should do is yield the generators from those calls instead of calling them directly.
